WHAT HAPPENED. We reported that his proposed Theorem A cannot hold as stated, because the
conjunction of neutral terminal objects, terminal essential surjectivity and
pi_0^pre(P)(X) = Lambda_0 forces H^1(X,A) = 0 - the very hypothesis he had listed as not worth
removing. He has confirmed it: "Your no-go proposition is correct... You have not misread the
literal conjunction of the conditions I proposed. That conjunction was too strong." Every
substantive step of our argument was accepted.

He also STRENGTHENED our result, and the manuscript should carry his stronger form:

  Terminal torsor obstruction. Let iota : P -> L = aP be the stackification of an A-banded
  prestack and let lambda be a neutral component of L. If P_lambda(X) -> L[lambda](X) is
  essentially surjective, then it is an EQUIVALENCE of groupoids, because stackification of a
  prestack is fully faithful - its morphism presheaves are already sheaves, so stackification
  adds no morphisms between existing objects. Consequently pi_0(P_lambda(X)) is a principal
  homogeneous set under H^1(X,A), and the automorphism group of every object is H^0(X,A). In
  particular, if the source has one isomorphism class, then H^1(X,A) = 0.

TASK 1 - record the obstruction in the manuscript, in his stronger form, with the fully
faithful stackification argument made explicit. Attribute the strengthening to the referee
process only in the sense of correctness, not by naming anyone; state it as a theorem of the
paper. Include his qualification about what "in the component lambda" must mean: all source
objects whose images have sheaf-component lambda. Also record the coefficient subtlety he
notes: Hom(H_1(N,Z), A) = 0 is weaker and coefficient-dependent than H^1(N,Z) = 0, so the
manuscript's H^1 = 0 hypothesis can sometimes be removed while keeping the original terminal
clauses - but not when H^1(X,A) = 0 and at least one neutral label exists. And note that the
all-non-neutral case Lambda_0 = empty is unaffected, both terminal fibres being empty with
essential surjectivity vacuous.

TASK 2 - adopt FORMULATION A, which he identifies as the correct replacement for the intended
presentation-independent marked realization. Do NOT mix it with formulation B; he states
explicitly that the two must not be mixed.

Formulation A retains terminal essential surjectivity and abandons pi_0^pre(P)(X) = Lambda_0,
replacing it by a marking map
    m : pi_0^pre(P(X)) -> Lambda_0
such that m^{-1}(lambda) is a principal homogeneous set under H^1(X,A) for every lambda in
Lambda_0. At groupoid level, after choosing one neutralizing object per component:
    P_lambda(X) ~= L[lambda](X) ~= Tors_A(X)   for lambda in Lambda_0,
    pi_0 P_lambda(X) is an H^1(X,A)-torsor,
    Aut(p) ~= H^0(X,A) for every object p,
    P_lambda(X) = L[lambda](X) = empty for non-neutral lambda,
so that
    pi_0^pre(P(X)) ~= disjoint union over lambda in Lambda_0 of T_lambda,
with each T_lambda an H^1(X,A)-torsor. He says this is the formulation that genuinely
"identifies exactly the unavoidable H^1 and H^0 ambiguities". Take the exact statement from his
report.

Record for completeness, without adopting it, that formulation B keeps pi_0^pre(P)(X) =
Lambda_0 but must drop terminal essential surjectivity, replacing it with
im(pi_0^pre(P(X)) -> pi_0^sh(L)(X)) = Lambda_0; that it is a pointed-atlas or
chosen-neutralization theorem, valid but weaker and more presentation-relative, with the
distinguished terminal object noncanonical when H^1 = 0. And record his impossibility remark:
when Lambda_0 is nonempty there is NO third formulation retaining all three of
pi_0^pre(P)(X) = Lambda_0, terminal essential surjectivity, and H^1(X,A) = 0.

BE HONEST ABOUT THE TIER. He says elsewhere in the same report that this correction does not
materially raise the paper's tier, and identifies the tier-relevant result as the full
localization. Do not present formulation A as a tier jump. State it as what it is: the correct

What remains beyond the calculations you report
Strict compatibility for a fixed composable chain of refinement maps is necessary but does not by itself establish presentation independence. The proof still has to handle:


two different refinement maps between the same covers;


two different common refinements;


arbitrary refinement zigzags;


passage to further refinement in the definition of 1- and 2-morphisms;


the proof that every marked banded equivalence is represented by a gauge after common refinement;


the identification of the resulting gauge groupoid with H1 and H0, rather than merely a map from ÄŒech classes into those groups.


This is exactly the point at which the present manuscript currently stops: it says that homological-image data are transported along specified comparisons but that no canonical identification along distinct comparison zigzags is asserted.  The conclusion likewise says that the appendix records naturality only along specified comparison words and does not prove path independence. 
Closing that gap is the part that would make the refinement theorem substantive.

5. Should the no-go proposition appear in the paper?
Yes, but not as a principal tier-raising theorem.
Its best form is the strengthened terminal torsor obstruction above. It would serve three useful purposes:


It prevents the three terminal levels
Ï€0preâ€‹P(X),Ï€0â€‹(L(X)),Ï€0shâ€‹(L)(X)
from being conflated.


It explains the otherwise hidden degree-one role of the hypothesis H1â€‹(N,Z)=0 in Theorem 4.2.


It states the exact terminal-fibre correction required when that hypothesis is removed.


The proposition is nevertheless a direct corollary of standard neutral-gerbe and torsor theory. It is not, by itself, a new high-tier result.
Effect on the paperâ€™s ceiling
The no-go has three different effects:


It does not lower the validity of the current Theorem 4.2, because the current H1â€‹(N,Z)=0 hypothesis supplies the required H1(X,A)=0.


It invalidates the literal strong form of my proposed Theorem A. The original three-way conjunction cannot be retained.


It does not eliminate the intended tier-raising route. The route must be reformulated as the refinement-localized marked ÄŒech-gerbe theorem above, with the terminal H1-torsor retained rather than collapsed.


The disconnected-overlap formula plus strict functoriality for chosen refinements, standing alone, would be a useful technical extension but would not materially raise the paperâ€™s tier. The tier-relevant result is the full localization/biequivalence statement with exact H2, H1, and H0 layers and with no connected-overlap or H1â€‹=0 restriction.
So the final diagnosis is:

Your obstruction is correct; the strong terminal-fibre formulation was overconstrained. The viable theorem is not a singleton-terminal-prestack realization but a refinement-localized 2-groupoid realization whose neutral terminal fibres are H1-torsors with H0 stabilizers.

\title[Finite-Site Component Gerbes]{Finite-Site Component Gerbes and Homological Images}
\author{Haobo Ma}
\address[Haobo Ma]{AELF PTE LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1, 8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981}
\email[Haobo Ma]{auric@aelf.io}

\author{Wenlin Zhang}
\address[Wenlin Zhang]{National University of Singapore, Singapore}
\email[Wenlin Zhang]{e1327962@u.nus.edu}

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
For a stack, Giraud's component construction identifies the full substack
over a global component section as its maximal component gerbe.  We apply
this standard construction to a banded prestack lift of a presheaf on a
finite site.  Under slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity,
matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutrality of every
global component gerbe.  A representative-rigidity theorem shows that a
strictly pullback-stable choice of lift representatives forces terminal
effectivity when the band has vanishing first cohomology.  On a finite good
cover with connected nonempty intersections and $H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0$, we
give a self-contained Cech-cocycle prestack construction simultaneously
controlling the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutral labels, and
prescribed evaluation maps $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A$.  UCT and elementary
subgroup arithmetic yield the aggregate quotients.  For the selected
open-star sites of $\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$, a nonzero finite abelian group
$G$ occurs as the two-component comparison kernel with complementary nonzero
images exactly when $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not cyclic of prime-power
order.  This is a realization corollary for supplied lift data and does not
transfer to bare empirical models.
\end{abstract}

\keywords{finite sites, component gerbes, banded prestacks, good covers, homological images, universal coefficient theorem}
\subjclass[2020]{18F20, 18G50, 55N30}

\title[Technical Appendix]{Technical Appendix for\\
  \emph{Finite-Site Component Gerbes and Homological Images}}
\author{Haobo Ma}
\address[Haobo Ma]{AELF PTE LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1, 8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981}
\email[Haobo Ma]{auric@aelf.io}

\author{Wenlin Zhang}
\address[Wenlin Zhang]{National University of Singapore, Singapore}
\email[Wenlin Zhang]{e1327962@u.nus.edu}

\begin{document}

\begin{abstract}
This technical appendix contains material auxiliary to the article
\emph{Finite-Site Component Gerbes and Homological Images}.  It records the
freely generated presentation-comparison groupoid and the corresponding UCT
naturality calculation, gives finite-group and Ext-only examples, and states
a narrow lower-language separation example that motivates treating lift data
as supplied input.  The article itself contains the complete proofs of
terminal rigidity and simultaneous prescribed realization.
\end{abstract}

\maketitle
\appendix
\input{sec_appendix}

\end{document}

\section{Introduction}\label{sec:introduction}

A stack in groupoids has a sheaf of connected components.  A global section
of that sheaf need not be represented by a global object of the stack, but it
does select a maximal subgerbe.  This is Giraud's component-gerbe construction
\cite[III, Prop.~2.1.5.3]{Giraud1971}.  The component is represented by a
global object precisely when its gerbe is neutral, or equivalently when its
Giraud \(H^2\)-class vanishes.  These classical facts are the starting point
of the article.

Our setting is deliberately finite and presentation-relative.  We fix a site
with terminal object, a presheaf \(F\), an abelian-banded prestack lift
\(\mathfrak P\), and its stackification \(\mathfrak L\).  The connected
components of \(\mathfrak P\) recover \(F\), while those of
\(\mathfrak L\) recover the sheafification \(aF\).  If \(F\) is separated on
the terminal slice and stackification is essentially surjective on the
terminal fibre, a strict matching family without an amalgamation occurs
exactly when \((aF)(a)\) is nonempty and every component gerbe selected by a
section of \((aF)(a)\) is non-neutral.  This is a comparison between a strict
presheaf predicate and standard gerbe theory, not a new classification of
gerbes.

The first paper-specific point is a rigidity statement for a particularly
strong kind of prestack presentation.  Suppose one selects a representative
of every presheaf component and requires those representatives to be stable
under pullback as literal equalities with respect to a split cleavage.  Under
slice separatedness, terminal essential surjectivity then forces the
sheafification unit
\[
 F(a)\longrightarrow(aF)(a)
\]
to be surjective.  Conversely, when \(H^1\) of the band vanishes,
surjectivity makes the Isom torsor between a selected representative and an
arbitrary terminal stack object trivial.  The result is a three-way
equivalence between terminal essential surjectivity, bijectivity of the
terminal sheafification unit, and the sheaf gluing condition at the terminal
object.  The proof in \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity} distinguishes the
strict-representative step from the classical \(H^1\)-torsor step.

The second point is an explicit finite-label prestack packaging.  Let
\(\mathcal U\) be a finite good cover with connected nerve \(N\), let \(A\)
be a finite abelian group, assume \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\), and prescribe a
finite family
\[
 \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A.
\]
UCT selects classes in \(H^2(N,A)\), and the standard Cech construction
realizes each class by an \(A\)-banded gerbe
\cite{Breen2006,Moerdijk2002}.  The construction in
\Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction} assembles these gerbes
into one prestack while controlling four features simultaneously:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item the presheaf and sheaf of component labels;
\item terminal essential surjectivity of stackification;
\item the exact set of neutral and non-neutral labels;
\item the evaluation maps and their image subgroups.
\end{enumerate}
The full construction and all four verifications are part of the article.

There is a coefficient hypothesis which cannot be suppressed.  For an
ordinary nerve to index the Cech cochain complex with one copy of \(A\) per
simplex, every nonempty finite overlap must be connected and the coefficient
trivializations must be coherent.  On a disconnected overlap \(U_J\), the
section group is \(A^{\pi_0(U_J)}\), information which the ordinary nerve
does not contain.  We impose the connected-overlap hypothesis explicitly in
\eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}.  The open-star covers used for the
wedge application have contractible nonempty intersections and satisfy it.

We also separate the formal algebra from the realization.  For a class
\(\omega\in H^2(N,A)\), put
\[
 K_\omega=\operatorname{Im}
 \bigl(H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}_\omega}A\bigr),
 \qquad Q_\omega=A/K_\omega.
\]
UCT naturality, quotient initiality, character annihilators, and the Ext
kernel are collected in one proposition.  For finitely many component
classes, the two aggregate quotients are
\[
 A/\bigcap_vK_v,
 \qquad A/\sum_vK_v,
\]
and their comparison is the elementary exact sequence
\[
 0\longrightarrow
 \frac{\sum_vK_v}{\bigcap_vK_v}
 \longrightarrow
 A/\bigcap_vK_v
 \longrightarrow
 A/\sum_vK_v
 \longrightarrow0.
\]
No independent information-theoretic claim is made for the cardinalities of
these fibres.

The finite-group endpoint is correspondingly stated as a realization
corollary.  On the all-subdivision open-star basis of a finite model of
\(\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2\), a nonzero finite abelian group \(G\) is
realized as the comparison kernel for two complementary nonzero component
images exactly when
\[
 d(G)\le2\beta
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 G\text{ is not cyclic of prime-power order}.
\]
The proof combines the prescribed-image prestack with the elementary fact
that the indecomposable finite abelian groups are the cyclic prime-power
groups.  It is not a classification attached to an arbitrary space,
presentation, or empirical model.

\subsection*{Inputs, standard steps, and paper-specific steps}

The logical order of the construction is
\[
\begin{array}{c}
(\mathcal C,F,\mathfrak P,\mathscr A)\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize stackification}\\
\mathfrak L,\ \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize pull back over }v\in(aF)(a)\\
\mathfrak G_v,\ [\mathfrak G_v]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\\
\big\downarrow\text{\scriptsize selected finite presentation and UCT}\\
\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_v}:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A,\quad
K_v,\quad Q_v.
\end{array}
\]
The first line is supplied data.  The component gerbe in the third line is
Giraud's standard maximal subgerbe over a component section.  Its neutrality
criterion and transport under a banded equivalence are also standard.  The
last line uses standard Cech comparison and UCT after a finite presentation
has been selected.

The article-specific statements concern restrictions and simultaneous
control across this sequence.  The rigidity theorem identifies a strict
representative hypothesis that collapses a possible terminal gluing failure
when \(H^1\) vanishes.  The prescribed-realization theorem constructs one
prestack in which the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutrality set, and
finite family of UCT evaluations are fixed together.  The wedge result is
then a corollary obtained by combining that construction with finite-group
arithmetic.  It is not presented as a separate classification mechanism.

This division also explains the theorem count.  Component-gerbe existence,
neutrality, banded naturality, UCT, duality, subgroup sums and intersections,
and quotient-fibre cardinality are consolidated rather than restated as
independent theorems.  Numbered statements are retained only where they
serve one of three roles:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
\item record the standard component-gerbe package with its exact Giraud
      antecedent;
\item state a paper-specific comparison, rigidity, or simultaneous
      realization assertion;
\item isolate an application boundary or the wedge realization endpoint.
\end{enumerate}

There are likewise two distinct uses of cohomology.  The Giraud class
\[
 [\mathfrak G_v]\in H^2(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
\]
decides neutrality of the full component gerbe.  After a finite connected
good-cover presentation with constant coefficient group \(A\) is fixed, its
image under
\[
 H^2(N,A)\longrightarrow
 \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A)
\]
measures only evaluation on integral \(2\)-cycles.  A nonzero Ext class can
be invisible to this map.  The homological image is therefore a quotient
construction applied to a supplied gerbe class, not a complete obstruction
theory for neutrality.

\subsection*{Scope and relation to the companion manuscript}

The lift data in this article are supplied.  They are not constructed from
teams, logical models, or empirical probability tables.  A bare empirical
model has a canonical split stack lift whose component gerbes are neutral.
The Abramsky--Mansfield--Barbosa obstruction is instead a relative Cech
\(H^1\)-torsor class indexed by a local section
\cite{AbramskyMansfieldSoaresBarbosa2012,Caru2017}.  The empty-domain argument
in \Cref{thm:empirical-model-boundary} prevents a universal recovery of those
classes from a global-component-section map.

The companion manuscript
\cite{MaZhang2026ConditionalGluingFailure} uses meet-semilattice sites.  The
overlap with the present pullback-site treatment is as follows.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.31\textwidth}
                >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.27\textwidth}
                >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.30\textwidth}}
\textbf{Topic here} & \textbf{Companion location} & \textbf{Status here}\\
\hline
Matching versus non-neutral components & Theorem 5.20 & Pullback-site
reformulation with the dependency chain displayed\\
Quotient initiality & Theorem 6.13 & Standard finite-nerve UCT clause in
\Cref{prop:uct-aggregate-package}\\
Component obstruction framework & Section 5 & Standard Giraud construction,
cited at theorem level\\
Terminal representative rigidity & No identical statement & Full narrow
no-go proof in \Cref{thm:canonical-lift-rigidity}\\
Finite-label prescribed realization & No identical package & Simultaneous
control theorem in \Cref{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}\\
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The table identifies overlap rather than claiming separate priority for the
first three rows.

\subsection*{Organization}

Section~\ref{sec:preliminaries} fixes the minimal presheaf and prestack
interface.  Section~\ref{sec:gerbe-obstruction} records the standard Giraud
component package, proves the comparison criterion, and gives the full
terminal-rigidity proof.  Section~\ref{sec:homological-visibility} proves the
connected-overlap, basis-site, and derived comparisons before constructing
the prescribed finite-label prestack.  Section~\ref{sec:homological-images}
collects the UCT and aggregate consequences.  The wedge application and the
empirical-model boundary occupy Sections~\ref{sec:wedge-application} and
\ref{sec:empirical-boundary}.  The separately compiled technical appendix
contains only presentation bookkeeping, extended examples, and the narrow
lower-language boundary argument; the proofs needed to certify the principal
claims are all contained here.

\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}

The article starts with supplied finite-site data: a presheaf, an
abelian-banded prestack lift, and its stackification.  Giraud's component
construction then assigns the maximal component gerbe to each global section
of the component sheaf.  The use made of this standard input is narrow: under
slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity it compares the
strict presheaf predicate of matching without amalgamation with non-neutrality
of every global component.  Representative-rigidity shows why a strictly
pullback-stable choice of lift representatives is too restrictive in the
basic \(H^1\)-vanishing regime.

On a finite good-cover site, the connected-overlap hypothesis is what permits
the ordinary nerve to carry the full constant-coefficient Cech complex.  The
all-subdivision basis comparison identifies the generated site with the
ordinary sheaf theory of the underlying polyhedron.  Within this fixed
setting, the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe construction can be packaged into a
single prestack whose component presheaf, terminal behavior, neutrality
pattern, and evaluation images are prescribed simultaneously.

UCT and elementary subgroup arithmetic then yield the aggregate quotients.
For the selected open-star covers of
\(\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2\), the two-component realization criterion is
\[
 d(G)\le2\beta
 \quad\text{and}\quad
 G\text{ is not cyclic of prime-power order}.
\]
This is a sharp corollary of the selected realization, not a new
classification principle for finite abelian groups or gerbes.

Bare empirical models provide only the support presheaf and its canonical
split stack, whose component gerbes are neutral.  Their relative degree-one
obstructions are indexed differently and are not recovered by the
component-section map.  A non-split banded lift must therefore be supplied
before the finite-site results apply.

The resulting contribution has a definite boundary.  The following parts
are classical inputs:
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{component over }v\text{ is a gerbe}
 &\text{Giraud III.2.1.5.3},\\
\text{neutrality}\Longleftrightarrow[\mathfrak G_v]=0
 &\text{abelian-banded gerbe classification},\\
\text{finite Cech class}\longrightarrow\text{UCT evaluation}
 &\text{Leray comparison and UCT},\\
\text{aggregate exact sequence}
 &\text{subgroup and quotient arithmetic.}
\end{array}
\]
They are stated together so that the hypotheses used by the applications
remain visible, not as separate advances.

The two proofs requiring construction beyond those inputs are terminal
rigidity and prescribed simultaneous realization.  The first identifies
where literal pullback stability removes a descent cocycle and where
\(H^1\)-vanishing removes the remaining Isom torsor.  The second constructs
the label presheaf and twisted groupoid fibres, verifies strict restrictions
and the genuine constant band, computes stackification components and
terminal objects, and recovers the selected Cech cocycles.  Their full proofs
make the claimed scope independently checkable within the article.

The technical appendix has a limited role.  Its presentation groupoid records
naturality only along specified comparison words and does not assert path
independence.  Its finite computations illustrate the UCT and subgroup
formulas.  Its lower-language example explains why the finite-site lift is
treated as input but does not construct that lift.

No crossed-module comparison is pursued: the present arguments begin after a
gerbe \(H^2\)-class and its Cech representative are supplied, and contain
neither the source-side degree-three chain model nor the comparison cochain
required to derive such a class from crossed-module data.


 succeeded in 186ms:
"""Finite and symbolic checks for the claims in oracle_sprint_A9_r1.md.

These checks verify only the algebraic consequences of UCT naturality and
NWW's displayed Cechization formula.  They do not prove either open
crossed-module comparison in NWW, Problems 8.1(b) and 8.2.
"""

import argparse
import sys
from contextlib import redirect_stdout
from fractions import Fraction
from functools import reduce
from io import StringIO
from itertools import combinations, product
from math import gcd
from pathlib import Path


def _continuous_three_cocycle(a, b, c):
    """A normalized continuous inhomogeneous 3-cocycle on (R,+)."""

    return a * b * c


def _group_coboundary(w, x, y, z):
    f = _continuous_three_cocycle
    return (
        f(x, y, z)
        - f(w + x, y, z)
        + f(w, x + y, z)
        - f(w, x, y + z)
        + f(w, x, y)
    )


def _tau(g, h, k, x):
    f = _continuous_three_cocycle
    return (
        f(h, k - h, x - k)
        - f(g, k - g, x - k)
        + f(g, h - g, x - h)
    )


def _tau_rewrite(g, h, k, x):
    f = _continuous_three_cocycle
    return f(g, h - g, k - h) + f(h - g, k - h, x - k)


def check_real_cechization_identities():
    values = tuple(Fraction(i, 2) for i in range(-4, 5))
    group_cases = 0
    tau_cases = 0
    cech_cases = 0

    for w, x, y, z in product(values, repeat=4):
        assert _group_coboundary(w, x, y, z) == 0
        group_cases += 1

    for g, h, k, x in product(values, repeat=4):
        assert _tau(g, h, k, x) == _tau_rewrite(g, h, k, x)
        tau_cases += 1

    for g, h, k, ell, x in product(values, repeat=5):
        cech_delta = (
            _tau(h, k, ell, x)
            - _tau(g, k, ell, x)
            + _tau(g, h, ell, x)
            - _tau(g, h, k, x)
        )
        assert cech_delta == 0
        cech_cases += 1

    return {
        "group_cocycle_cases": group_cases,
        "tau_rewrite_cases": tau_cases,
        "cech_cocycle_cases": cech_cases,
    }


def _divisors(n):
    return tuple(d for d in range(1, n + 1) if n % d == 0)


def _cyclic_subgroup(n, generators):
    divisor = reduce(gcd, generators, n)
    return frozenset(range(0, n, divisor))


def check_finite_quotient_claims(max_modulus=36):
    factorization_cases = 0
    exact_sequence_cases = 0

    for n in range(1, max_modulus + 1):
        subgroups = tuple(_cyclic_subgroup(n, (d,)) for d in _divisors(n))

        for subgroup in subgroups:
            for quotient_modulus in _divisors(n):
                units = tuple(
                    u for u in range(quotient_modulus) if gcd(u, quotient_modulus) == 1
                ) or (0,)
                for unit in units:
                    kills_image = all((unit * x) % quotient_modulus == 0 for x in subgroup)
                    factors_through = subgroup <= frozenset(
                        x
                        for x in range(n)
                        if (unit * x) % quotient_modulus == 0
                    )
                    pushed_evaluation_is_zero = kills_image
                    assert kills_image == factors_through == pushed_evaluation_is_zero
                    factorization_cases += 1

        for first, second, third in product(subgroups, repeat=3):
            intersection = first & second & third
            subgroup_sum = _cyclic_subgroup(n, tuple(first | second | third))
            assert intersection <= subgroup_sum

            labelled_quotient_order = n // len(intersection)
            common_quotient_order = n // len(subgroup_sum)
            kernel_order = len(subgroup_sum) // len(intersection)
            assert labelled_quotient_order == kernel_order * common_quotient_order
            exact_sequence_cases += 1

    return {
        "factorization_cases": factorization_cases,
        "exact_sequence_cases": exact_sequence_cases,
    }


def _elements(moduli):
    return tuple(product(*(range(modulus) for modulus in moduli)))


def _add(left, right, moduli):
    return tuple((x + y) % modulus for x, y, modulus in zip(left, right, moduli))


def _generated_subgroup(moduli, generators):
    zero = tuple(0 for _ in moduli)
    generated = {zero}
    frontier = [zero]
    while frontier:
        current = frontier.pop()
        for generator in generators:
            candidate = _add(current, generator, moduli)
            if candidate not in generated:
                generated.add(candidate)
                frontier.append(candidate)
    return frozenset(generated)


def _all_subgroups(moduli):
    elements = _elements(moduli)
    zero = tuple(0 for _ in moduli)
    subgroups = []
    for size in range(1, len(elements) + 1):
        for subset_tuple in combinations(elements, size):
            subset = frozenset(subset_tuple)
            if zero not in subset:
                continue
            if all(_add(x, y, moduli) in subset for x in subset for y in subset):
                subgroups.append(subset)
    return tuple(subgroups)


def _minimum_generator_number(moduli, subgroup):
    elements = tuple(subgroup)
    for beta in range(len(elements) + 1):
        if any(
            _generated_subgroup(moduli, generators) == subgroup
            for generators in product(elements, repeat=beta)
        ):
            return beta
    raise AssertionError("finite subgroup has no finite generating tuple")


def check_generator_bound_classification():
    moduli_list = ((2,), (3,), (4,), (2, 2), (2, 3), (2, 2, 2), (2, 4))
    classification_cases = 0

    for moduli in moduli_list:
        elements = _elements(moduli)
        subgroups = _all_subgroups(moduli)
        minimum_generators = {
            subgroup: _minimum_generator_number(moduli, subgroup)
            for subgroup in subgroups
        }

        for beta in range(4):
            homomorphism_images = {
                _generated_subgroup(moduli, generators)
                for generators in product(elements, repeat=beta)
            }
            bounded_generator_subgroups = {
                subgroup
                for subgroup, number in minimum_generators.items()
                if number <= beta
            }
            assert homomorphism_images == bounded_generator_subgroups
            classification_cases += len(subgroups)

    return {"groups": len(moduli_list), "classification_cases": classification_cases}


def pullback_strict_inclusion_example():
    # ev: H_r(S^r v S^r) = Z^2 -> Z/2 is projection to the first factor.
    ambient_image = frozenset({0, 1})
    # Pull back along the inclusion of the second sphere: t |-> (0,t).
    pullback_image = frozenset({0})
    assert pullback_image < ambient_image
    return {
        "ambient_image": ambient_image,
        "pullback_image": pullback_image,
        "ambient_quotient_order": 2 // len(ambient_image),
        "pullback_quotient_order": 2 // len(pullback_image),
    }


def action_lift_peiffer_counterexample():
    # G = K = C_2 and K-hat = C_2 x C_2 with boundary (a,b) |-> a.
    def boundary(element):
        return element[0]

    def action(group_element, element):
        a, b = element
        return (a, (b + group_element * a) % 2)

    def multiply(left, right):
        return ((left[0] + right[0]) % 2, (left[1] + right[1]) % 2)

    def inverse(element):
        return element  # Every element of C_2 x C_2 has order at most two.

    elements = tuple(product(range(2), repeat=2))
    covers_conjugation = all(
        boundary(action(g, element)) == boundary(element)
        for g in range(2)
        for element in elements
    )
    fixes_central_kernel = all(
        action(g, (0, z)) == (0, z) for g in range(2) for z in range(2)
    )
    peiffer_identity = all(
        action(boundary(left), right)
        == multiply(multiply(left, right), inverse(left))
        for left in elements
        for right in elements
    )
    assert covers_conjugation and fixes_central_kernel and not peiffer_identity
    return {
        "covers_conjugation": covers_conjugation,
        "fixes_central_kernel": fixes_central_kernel,
        "peiffer_identity": peiffer_identity,
    }


def _run_verification():
    real = check_real_cechization_identities()
    finite = check_finite_quotient_claims()
    generators = check_generator_bound_classification()
    pullback = pullback_strict_inclusion_example()
    peiffer = action_lift_peiffer_counterexample()
    print("PASS NWW Cechization formula checks:", real)
    print("PASS finite quotient checks:", finite)
    print("PASS generator-bound checks:", generators)
    print("PASS pullback direction check:", pullback)
    print("COUNTEREXAMPLE action lift does not imply Peiffer identity:", peiffer)
    print("OPEN comparisons intentionally unverified: NWW Problems 8.1(b), 8.2(a), 8.2(b)")


def main(argv=()):
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--output", type=Path)
    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
    if args.output is None:
        _run_verification()
        return

    capture = StringIO()
    with redirect_stdout(capture):
        _run_verification()
    report = capture.getvalue()
    args.output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    args.output.write_text(report, encoding="utf-8", newline="\n")
    print(report, end="")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main(sys.argv[1:])

import importlib.util
from pathlib import Path
import unittest


SCRIPT = Path(__file__).with_name("verify_A9_r1.py")


def load_verifier():
    spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location("verify_A9_r1", SCRIPT)
    if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
        raise RuntimeError(f"cannot load {SCRIPT}")
    module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
    spec.loader.exec_module(module)
    return module


class OracleA9R1VerificationTests(unittest.TestCase):
    def test_real_cechization_identities(self):
        verifier = load_verifier()
        result = verifier.check_real_cechization_identities()
        self.assertGreater(result["group_cocycle_cases"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(result["tau_rewrite_cases"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(result["cech_cocycle_cases"], 0)

    def test_finite_quotient_and_exact_sequence_claims(self):
        verifier = load_verifier()
        result = verifier.check_finite_quotient_claims(max_modulus=36)
        self.assertGreater(result["factorization_cases"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(result["exact_sequence_cases"], 0)

    def test_generator_bound_classification(self):
        verifier = load_verifier()
        result = verifier.check_generator_bound_classification()
        self.assertGreater(result["groups"], 0)
        self.assertGreater(result["classification_cases"], 0)

    def test_pullback_is_not_image_equality(self):
        verifier = load_verifier()
        self.assertEqual(
            verifier.pullback_strict_inclusion_example(),
            {
                "ambient_image": frozenset({0, 1}),
                "pullback_image": frozenset({0}),
                "ambient_quotient_order": 1,
                "pullback_quotient_order": 2,
            },
        )

    def test_action_lift_need_not_satisfy_peiffer_identity(self):
        verifier = load_verifier()
        result = verifier.action_lift_peiffer_counterexample()
        self.assertTrue(result["covers_conjugation"])
        self.assertTrue(result["fixes_central_kernel"])
        self.assertFalse(result["peiffer_identity"])


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

 succeeded in 244ms:
 240: 
 241: A nonempty intersection of open stars is the open star of the simplex spanned
 242: by the corresponding vertices, hence is contractible.  Barycentric
 243: subdivision refines the preceding cover, and every finite basis cover is
 244: refined by a sufficiently late subdivision cover; the sequence is therefore
 245: filtered and cofinal.  The basic intersections are acyclic for the constant
 246: sheaf, so (ii), the sheaf-category equivalence, and cofinality give
 247: \[
 248:  \varinjlim_m\check H^2(\mathcal U^{(m)},A)
 249:  \cong H^2(\mathcal B_K,A)
 250:  \cong H^2(X,A).
 251: \]
 252: This proves every asserted comparison, including the passage from the
 253: all-subdivision generated site to the usual topological site.
 254: 
 255: The order of the argument is essential.  Contractibility supplies
 256: connectedness and acyclicity on each selected cover.  The basis comparison
 257: identifies sheaves on the generated site with sheaves on \(X\).  Cofinality
 258: then identifies the colimit over subdivision covers with the full Cech
 259: colimit.  Only after these three checks is the final derived-cohomology
 260: identification justified.
 261: \end{proof}
 262: 
 263: We turn to the paper-specific packaging of the standard Cech-cocycle gerbe
 264: construction.  The point is not that a class in \(H^2\) can be represented
 265: by a gerbe; that is classical.  The content is simultaneous control of the
 266: component presheaf, terminal essential surjectivity, neutral and non-neutral
 267: labels, and the images of the evaluation homomorphisms.
 268: 
 269: \begin{theorem}[Finite-site realization of prescribed componentwise evaluation maps]
 270: \label{thm:prescribed-component-image-construction}
 271: Let \(\mathcal U=\{U_i\}_{i\in I}\) be a finite good cover satisfying
 272: \eqref{eq:connected-overlap-hypothesis}, with connected nerve \(N\), and let
 273: \(A\) be a finite abelian group.  Assume
 274: \[
 275:  H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0.
 276: \]
 277: Let \(\Lambda\) be a finite nonempty set and prescribe homomorphisms
 278: \[
 279:  \phi_\lambda:H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\longrightarrow A
 280:  \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda).
 281: \]
 282: Put \(\Lambda_0=\{\lambda:\phi_\lambda=0\}\).  Then there are a presheaf
 283: \(F\) on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) and an
 284: \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack lift
 285: \(\mathfrak P\), with stackification \(\mathfrak L\), such that:
 286: \begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*),nosep]
 287: \item \(F(a)=\Lambda_0\), \((aF)(a)\cong\Lambda\), and
 288:       \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\cong\Lambda\);
 289: \item the component gerbe \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) has class
 290:       \(\omega_\lambda\in H^2(N,A)\) satisfying
 291:       \[
 292:       \operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda,
 293:       \qquad
 294:       \operatorname{Im}(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda})
 295:       =\operatorname{Im}(\phi_\lambda),
 296:       \]
 297:       and \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\) is neutral exactly when
 298:       \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\);
 299: \item \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially surjective, and
 300:       its terminal objects occur exactly in the components indexed by
 301:       \(\Lambda_0\);
 302: \item \(F\) has a strict matching family on \(\mathcal U\), and
 303:       \[
 304:       \operatorname{MWA}(F,a)
 305:       \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 306:       \Lambda_0=\varnothing.
 307:       \]
 308: \end{enumerate}
 309: \end{theorem}
 310: 
 311: \begin{proof}
 312: The universal coefficient sequence and \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) give a natural
 313: isomorphism
 314: \begin{equation}\label{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism}
 315:  \eta_A:H^2(N,A)\xrightarrow{\sim}
 316:  \operatorname{Hom}(H_2(N,\mathbb Z),A).
 317: \end{equation}
 318: For every \(\lambda\), let \(\omega_\lambda\) be the inverse image of
 319: \(\phi_\lambda\).  Choose a normalized simplicial cocycle
 320: \(\alpha_\lambda\in Z^2(N,A)\) representing it, choosing
 321: \(\alpha_\lambda=0\) when \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\).  Extend it alternately
 322: to ordered triples and set it to zero on triples with a repeated index.
 323: 
 324: The choice \(\alpha_\lambda=0\) for a zero map is legitimate because
 325: \(\eta_A\) is injective: \(\phi_\lambda=0\) implies
 326: \(\omega_\lambda=0\).  Conversely, a nonzero prescribed map has a nonzero
 327: class.  For pairwise distinct indices, alternating extension means
 328: \[
 329:  \alpha_{\lambda,\sigma(i)\sigma(j)\sigma(k)}
 330:  =\operatorname{sgn}(\sigma)\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}.
 331: \]
 332: On every ordered \(3\)-simplex, the cocycle equation is
 333: \begin{equation}\label{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}
 334:  \alpha_{\lambda,jkl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 335:  +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}-\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}=0.
 336: \end{equation}
 337: These are the only coefficient identities used in the groupoid
 338: construction.
 339: 
 340: Define the component presheaf by
 341: \[
 342:  F(W)=
 343:  \begin{cases}
 344:  \Lambda,&W\ne a,\varnothing\text{ is a nonempty intersection object},\\
 345:  \Lambda_0,&W=a,\\
 346:  \{*\},&W=\varnothing.
 347:  \end{cases}
 348: \]
 349: Restrictions among proper nonempty intersections are identities,
 350: restrictions from \(a\) are the inclusion
 351: \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\), and restrictions to
 352: \(\varnothing\) are unique.  A matching family on the vertex cover has a
 353: single label on every connected component of the nerve.  Since \(N\) is
 354: connected, its label is a unique element of \(\Lambda\).  Consequently
 355: \begin{equation}\label{eq:label-sheafification}
 356:  (aF)(a)\cong\Lambda.
 357: \end{equation}
 358: 
 359: Here is the sheafification calculation in detail.  A family
 360: \((\lambda_i)_{i\in I}\) with \(\lambda_i\in F(U_i)=\Lambda\) is matching
 361: exactly when
 362: \[
 363:  \lambda_i=\lambda_j
 364:  \quad\text{for every edge }\{i,j\}\in N.
 365: \]
 366: No condition is imposed by an empty overlap because both restrictions land
 367: in the singleton \(F(\varnothing)\).  Edgewise equality propagates along
 368: paths in the connected \(1\)-skeleton of \(N\), so every matching family is
 369: constant.  Conversely, each constant family is matching.  It determines a
 370: section of \(aF\) over \(a\), and distinct constants remain distinct after
 371: restriction because every nonempty cover member retains its label.
 372: 
 373: Every cover in the generated pretopology is obtained from the vertex cover
 374: by pullback and transitivity.  On a nonempty proper intersection, a generated
 375: cover contains an identity, so it neither creates nor identifies labels.
 376: It follows that the vertex-cover calculation computes all terminal sections
 377: of the sheafification.  On proper nonempty objects,
 378: \((aF)(W)=\Lambda\), and on the initial object it is a singleton.
 379: 
 380: We next construct the prestack and verify its structure rather than citing a
 381: separate realization result.  Fix \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\).  Put
 382: \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\varnothing\).  For a nonempty proper intersection
 383: \(W\), let
 384: \[
 385:  I(W)=\{i\in I:W\subseteq U_i\}.
 386: \]
 387: The groupoid \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(W)\) has objects
 388: \(x_i^{\lambda,W}\) for \(i\in I(W)\), and
 389: \[
 390:  \operatorname{Hom}(x_i^{\lambda,W},x_j^{\lambda,W})
 391:  =A_{\mathcal U}(W)\cong A.
 392: \]
 393: Writing a morphism as \((b,i,j)\), define
 394: \begin{equation}\label{eq:twisted-composition}
 395:  (c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)
 396:  =(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},i,k).
 397: \end{equation}
 398: Normalization supplies identities and inverses.  Explicitly,
 399: \[
 400:  1_{x_i^{\lambda,W}}=(0,i,i),
 401:  \qquad
 402:  (b,i,j)^{-1}=(-b,j,i),
 403: \]
 404: because every cocycle coordinate with a repeated index is zero.  For three
 405: composable arrows, the two parenthesizations are
 406: \[
 407: \begin{aligned}
 408:  (d,k,l)\circ\bigl((c,j,k)\circ(b,i,j)\bigr)
 409:  &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
 410:        +\alpha_{\lambda,ikl},i,l),\\
 411:  \bigl((d,k,l)\circ(c,j,k)\bigr)\circ(b,i,j)
 412:  &=(b+c+d+\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}
 413:        +\alpha_{\lambda,ijl},i,l).
 414: \end{aligned}
 415: \]
 416: They agree by \eqref{eq:realization-cocycle-equation}.  Associativity is
 417: therefore exactly the cocycle identity
 418: \[
 419:  \alpha_{\lambda,ijk}+\alpha_{\lambda,ikl}
 420:  =\alpha_{\lambda,jkl}+\alpha_{\lambda,ijl}.
 421: \]
 422: If \(V\subseteq W\), then \(I(W)\subseteq I(V)\); restriction keeps the
 423: object index and applies the sheaf restriction to the coefficient.  These
 424: functors compose strictly.  Over \(\varnothing\) take the terminal groupoid.
 425: 
 426: To verify the restriction assertion, write
 427: \(\rho_{VW}:A_{\mathcal U}(W)\to A_{\mathcal U}(V)\).  The functor sends
 428: \[
 429:  x_i^{\lambda,W}\longmapsto x_i^{\lambda,V},
 430:  \qquad
 431:  (b,i,j)\longmapsto(\rho_{VW}(b),i,j).
 432: \]
 433: On nonempty connected intersections, the coherent trivializations identify
 434: \(\rho_{VW}\) with the identity of \(A\).  Hence
 435: \[
 436:  \rho_{VW}(b+c+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk})
 437:  =\rho_{VW}(b)+\rho_{VW}(c)+\alpha_{\lambda,ijk},
 438: \]
 439: so restriction preserves \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}.  If
 440: \(Z\subseteq V\subseteq W\), the equality
 441: \(\rho_{ZV}\rho_{VW}=\rho_{ZW}\) gives literal equality of the restriction
 442: functors.  This is strict functoriality, not functoriality only up to a
 443: cartesian comparison arrow.
 444: 
 445: For fixed \(i,j\in I(W)\), the presheaf of arrows between the corresponding
 446: objects on the slice over \(W\) is
 447: \[
 448:  V\longmapsto A_{\mathcal U}(V).
 449: \]
 450: It is the restriction of the genuine coefficient sheaf, and hence is a
 451: sheaf.  This verifies morphism descent for every twisted summand.
 452: Translation in the coefficient coordinate identifies
 453: \[
 454:  \underline{\operatorname{Aut}}(x_i^{\lambda,W})
 455:  \cong A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}.
 456: \]
 457: Changing \(i\) transports this identification through an arrow
 458: \((b,i,j)\); abelianness makes the induced conjugation on \(A\) trivial.
 459: The identifications therefore constitute an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banding.
 460: 
 461: For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), let \(\mathfrak P_\lambda\) be the neutral
 462: one-object \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded stack on every nonempty object and the
 463: terminal groupoid on \(\varnothing\).  This is a stack here, not merely a
 464: one-object prestack: on the slice over a nonempty formal intersection, every
 465: generated cover contains an identity, so the nonempty part of the topology
 466: is trivial; the empty cover forces value zero at \(\varnothing\).  Evaluation
 467: at the terminal slice object is exact, whence
 468: \(H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})=0\), and every object
 469: descent torsor is trivial.
 470: 
 471: Concretely, descent data for the distinguished local object on a cover
 472: \(\{V_r\to W\}\) are arrows represented by coefficients
 473: \(d_{rs}\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_{rs})\) satisfying
 474: \[
 475:  d_{rs}+d_{st}=d_{rt}.
 476: \]
 477: They form an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycle.  The vanishing just noted
 478: provides coefficients \(e_r\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_r)\) with
 479: \(d_{rs}=e_s-e_r\).  Adjusting the local identifications by \(e_r\) makes
 480: them agree and yields the global object.  Morphism descent is the ordinary
 481: sheaf condition for \(A_{\mathcal U}\).  Thus the neutral summand is already
 482: a stack on every relevant slice.
 483: 
 484: Set
 485: \[
 486:  \mathfrak P=\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda}\mathfrak P_\lambda
 487: \]
 488: fibrewise on nonempty objects, with a shared terminal fibre over
 489: \(\varnothing\).  For two objects in a twisted fibre, the isomorphism
 490: presheaf on the relevant slice is the restriction of the genuine sheaf
 491: \(A_{\mathcal U}\); it is therefore a sheaf.  Automorphism sheaves are the
 492: same sheaf, so \(\mathfrak P\) is an \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded prestack.
 493: Every nonempty proper fibre has connected components indexed by \(\Lambda\),
 494: the terminal fibre has components \(\Lambda_0\), and the empty fibre has one
 495: component.  Thus
 496: \[
 497:  \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)\cong F.
 498: \]
 499: Stackification and \eqref{eq:label-sheafification} prove (i).
 500: 
 501: There are two points in this coproduct verification.  First, over a nonempty
 502: object there are no arrows between different \(\lambda\)-summands, so
 503: isomorphism descent cannot change a label.  A matching object descent datum
 504: therefore lies in one summand on each connected part of a cover, and the
 505: connected nerve forces a single label.  Second, all summands restrict to the
 506: same terminal groupoid over \(\varnothing\).  This causes no identification
 507: on a nonempty overlap: the initial object has no arrow back to a nonempty
 508: object and its unique descent datum contributes no coefficient.
 509: 
 510: For a twisted summand, every two named objects are isomorphic, since their
 511: Hom set is the nonempty set \(A\).  Thus each such fibre is connected.
 512: The neutral summand has one object and is connected as well.  It follows
 513: objectwise that the component presheaf is exactly \(F\), including its
 514: restriction \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\) out of the terminal object.
 515: The standard compatibility of stackification with components gives
 516: \[
 517:  \pi_0(\mathfrak L)\cong aF,
 518: \]
 519: and the terminal component sections are the labels in \(\Lambda\).
 520: 
 521: On \(U_i\), choose \(x_i^{\lambda,U_i}\); on \(U_{ij}\), choose the arrow
 522: \((0,i,j)\).  By \eqref{eq:twisted-composition}, its triple-overlap defect is
 523: \[
 524:  (0,i,k)^{-1}(0,j,k)(0,i,j)
 525:  =\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}
 526: \]
 527: under the band identification.  The Cech representative of the component
 528: gerbe is therefore exactly \(\alpha_\lambda\).  The good-cover comparison
 529: in \Cref{prop:finite-site-comparison} identifies its Giraud class with
 530: \(\omega_\lambda\), and
 531: \eqref{eq:uct-realization-isomorphism} gives
 532: \(\operatorname{ev}_{\omega_\lambda}=\phi_\lambda\).  By the standard
 533: component-gerbe package, this gerbe is neutral exactly when its class is zero,
 534: equivalently when \(\phi_\lambda=0\).  This proves (ii).
 535: 
 536: To check the sign in the defect, use
 537: \[
 538:  (0,i,k)^{-1}=(0,k,i)
 539: \]
 540: and apply \eqref{eq:twisted-composition} twice.  Normalization cancels the
 541: terms with repeated indices, while alternating extension moves the remaining
 542: term to the ordered coordinate \(\alpha_{\lambda,ijk}\).  This is the same
 543: convention as \eqref{eq:giraud-triple-defect}.  Therefore the class obtained
 544: by stackification is not merely cohomologous to a class with the desired
 545: evaluation: its chosen vertex-cover representative is the initially
 546: selected cocycle \(\alpha_\lambda\).
 547: 
 548: The connected-overlap hypothesis enters here a second time.  It identifies
 549: the automorphism section on \(U_{ijk}\) with one element of \(A\), so the
 550: defect is an ordinary simplicial coordinate.  If \(U_{ijk}\) were
 551: disconnected, the same calculation would yield a tuple in
 552: \(A^{\pi_0(U_{ijk})}\), and the prescribed ordinary-nerve cocycle would not
 553: specify the entire defect.
 554: 
 555: For \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the neutral summand is already a stack and its
 556: terminal object maps to the corresponding terminal object of
 557: \(\mathfrak L\).  If \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), the component gerbe is
 558: non-neutral and therefore has no object over \(a\).  Hence every object of
 559: \(\mathfrak L(a)\) lies in a zero component and comes from
 560: \(\mathfrak P(a)\), proving (iii), including the vacuous case
 561: \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\).
 562: 
 563: When \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), an object of
 564: \(\mathfrak L(a)\) has some component label
 565: \(\lambda\in\Lambda\).  The component-gerbe package identifies the full
 566: substack at that label with \(\mathfrak G_\lambda\).  If
 567: \(\lambda\notin\Lambda_0\), non-neutrality says that this full substack has
 568: no terminal object, so the assumed object cannot have that label.  If
 569: \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the distinguished terminal object of the neutral
 570: summand of \(\mathfrak P\) maps to an object in the same component of
 571: \(\mathfrak L\).  Any other terminal object in that component is isomorphic
 572: to it because the neutral component is a gerbe.  This is precisely essential
 573: surjectivity, rather than equality of object sets.
 574: 
 575: Finally, assigning one fixed \(\lambda\in\Lambda\) to every \(U_i\) is a
 576: strict matching family: restrictions on nonempty overlaps are identities and
 577: those on empty overlaps are unique.  It has an amalgamation precisely when
 578: \(F(a)=\Lambda_0\) is nonempty.  This proves (iv) and completes the
 579: construction.
 580: 
 581: In terms of the definition from \Cref{sec:preliminaries}, the last
 582: equivalence uses only existence.  If \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\), every
 583: constant label gives a strict matching family and there is no global
 584: section, so matching without amalgamation holds.  If
 585: \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\), \(F(a)\) is nonempty and the predicate
 586: \(\operatorname{MWA}(F,a)\) is false, although matching families with
 587: nonzero labels may still fail individually to amalgamate.  The theorem does
 588: not claim that every matching family amalgamates in this case.
 589: \end{proof}
 590: 
 591: The gerbes in this proof are obtained from the standard Cech-cocycle
 592: construction.  What the theorem adds is the single finite-label prestack
 593: whose sheaf of components, terminal fibre, neutrality pattern, and homological
 594: images are all prescribed simultaneously.
 595: 
 596: \subsection*{The two terminal-fibre endpoints}
 597: 
 598: If every prescribed map is zero, then \(\Lambda_0=\Lambda\).  The component
 599: presheaf is already constant on every nonempty object, all summands are
 600: neutral, and the stackification has a terminal object in every global
 601: component.  The theorem reduces to a finite coproduct of split
 602: \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-banded stacks.  Matching families and global presheaf
 603: sections are both indexed by \(\Lambda\).
 604: 
 605: At the other endpoint, suppose every prescribed map is nonzero.  Then
 606: \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\), so
 607: \[
 608:  F(a)=\varnothing,\qquad
 609:  (aF)(a)=\Lambda,\qquad
 610:  \mathfrak P(a)=\varnothing.
 611: \]
 612: Every global component gerbe is non-neutral, hence
 613: \(\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\) as well.  The map
 614: \[
 615:  \mathfrak P(a)\longrightarrow\mathfrak L(a)
 616: \]
 617: is essentially surjective because both fibres are empty.  This is the
 618: vacuous terminal-essential-surjectivity case used by matching without
 619: amalgamation.  It is not a claim that the non-neutral global component
 620: sections have terminal objects; they do not.
 621: 
 622: For a mixed family, \(\mathfrak L(a)\) contains objects exactly in the
 623: components indexed by \(\Lambda_0\).  The nonzero labels still occur as
 624: sections of \(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\cong(aF)(a)\), and their full component
 625: substacks are locally inhabited gerbes without terminal objects.  This is
 626: the concrete distinction among the three terminal levels in
 627: \eqref{eq:three-terminal-levels}.
 628: 
 629: \subsection*{A three-label calculation}
 630: 
 631: Take a triangulated \(2\)-sphere and an open-star cover of a barycentric
 632: subdivision.  Its nerve \(N\) satisfies
 633: \[
 634:  H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0,\qquad H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\cong\mathbb Z.
 635: \]
 636: Let \(A=\mathbb Z/6\mathbb Z\), and take the label set
 637: \[
 638:  \Lambda=\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\}.
 639: \]
 640: Prescribe
 641: \[
 642: \begin{aligned}
 643:  \phi_{\mathtt n}(1)&=0,\\
 644:  \phi_{\mathtt p}(1)&=2,\\
 645:  \phi_{\mathtt q}(1)&=3.
 646: \end{aligned}
 647: \]
 648: Then
 649: \[
 650:  \Lambda_0=\{\mathtt n\},\qquad
 651:  K_{\mathtt n}=0,\qquad
 652:  K_{\mathtt p}=\langle2\rangle,\qquad
 653:  K_{\mathtt q}=\langle3\rangle.
 654: \]
 655: The three image groups have orders \(1,3,2\), respectively.  The UCT
 656: isomorphism chooses three classes in \(H^2(N,A)\cong A\), and normalized
 657: cocycles \(\alpha_{\mathtt n}=0\),
 658: \(\alpha_{\mathtt p}\), and \(\alpha_{\mathtt q}\) feed the twisted
 659: composition formula.
 660: 
 661: The component presheaf has
 662: \[
 663:  F(a)=\{\mathtt n\},
 664:  \qquad
 665:  F(U_J)=\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\}
 666: \]
 667: on every proper nonempty intersection.  Its sheafification has three
 668: terminal component sections.  Only the \(\mathtt n\)-component has a
 669: terminal stack object.  Therefore
 670: \[
 671:  \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
 672:  =\{\mathtt n,\mathtt p,\mathtt q\},
 673:  \qquad
 674:  \pi_0(\mathfrak L(a))=\{\mathtt n\}.
 675: \]
 676: The notation on the right means the set of isomorphism classes of actual
 677: terminal objects; it is smaller than the value at \(a\) of the component
 678: sheaf.
 679: 
 680: For the two nonzero labels,
 681: \[
 682:  Q_{\mathtt p}=A/\langle2\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z,
 683:  \qquad
 684:  Q_{\mathtt q}=A/\langle3\rangle\cong\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z.
 685: \]
 686: Their images are complementary:
 687: \[
 688:  \langle2\rangle\cap\langle3\rangle=0,
 689:  \qquad
 690:  \langle2\rangle+\langle3\rangle=A.
 691: \]
 692: If all three labels are aggregated, the zero image from
 693: \(\mathtt n\) keeps the intersection equal to zero, while the sum remains
 694: \(A\).  Hence
 695: \[
 696:  Q_{\mathrm{lab}}=A,\qquad Q_{\mathrm{com}}=0.
 697: \]
 698: 
 699: This mixed example does not satisfy matching without amalgamation because
 700: \(F(a)\ne\varnothing\).  It does contain matching families with labels

  67: \title[Finite-Site Component Gerbes]{Finite-Site Component Gerbes and Homological Images}
  68: \author{Haobo Ma}
  69: \address[Haobo Ma]{AELF PTE LTD., \#14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1, 8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981}
  70: \email[Haobo Ma]{auric@aelf.io}
  71: 
  72: \author{Wenlin Zhang}
  73: \address[Wenlin Zhang]{National University of Singapore, Singapore}
  74: \email[Wenlin Zhang]{e1327962@u.nus.edu}
  75: 
  76: \begin{document}
  77: 
  78: \begin{abstract}
  79: For a stack, Giraud's component construction identifies the full substack
  80: over a global component section as its maximal component gerbe.  We apply
  81: this standard construction to a banded prestack lift of a presheaf on a
  82: finite site.  Under slice separatedness and terminal essential surjectivity,
  83: matching without amalgamation is equivalent to non-neutrality of every
  84: global component gerbe.  A representative-rigidity theorem shows that a
  85: strictly pullback-stable choice of lift representatives forces terminal
  86: effectivity when the band has vanishing first cohomology.  On a finite good
  87: cover with connected nonempty intersections and $H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0$, we
  88: give a self-contained Cech-cocycle prestack construction simultaneously
  89: controlling the component presheaf, terminal fibre, neutral labels, and
  90: prescribed evaluation maps $H_2(N,\mathbb Z)\to A$.  UCT and elementary
  91: subgroup arithmetic yield the aggregate quotients.  For the selected
  92: open-star sites of $\bigvee_{j=1}^{\beta}S^2$, a nonzero finite abelian group
  93: $G$ occurs as the two-component comparison kernel with complementary nonzero
  94: images exactly when $d(G)\le2\beta$ and $G$ is not cyclic of prime-power
  95: order.  This is a realization corollary for supplied lift data and does not
  96: transfer to bare empirical models.
  97: \end{abstract}
  98: 
  99: \keywords{finite sites, component gerbes, banded prestacks, good covers, homological images, universal coefficient theorem}

 380: single element of \(A\), and compatibility along nonempty pullback
 381: overlaps is exactly equality of those local constants. If \(|V|\) is
 382: disconnected, the same sheaf axiom glues independently on the connected
 383: components of \(|V|\), giving \(\operatorname{LC}(|V|,A)\) rather than a
 384: single copy of \(A\).
 385: Cover members whose pullback is \(\emptyset\) impose no equality because
 386: \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\); when \(V=\emptyset\), the only covering
 387: is refined by the empty cover and the unique section is \(0\). This proves
 388: \textup{(ii)} without invoking the identity-restriction constant
 389: presheaf, and the same paragraph proves the explicit matching-family
 390: description in \textup{(iii)}.
 391: 
 392: For \textup{(iv)}, the displayed identification is an isomorphism
 393: \[
 394: \underline{\mathrm{Isom}}(x,y)\cong
 395: A_{\mathcal U}|_{\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W}
 396: \]
 397: of presheaves on the slice; the right-hand side is a sheaf by
 398: \textup{(ii)}. This is the automorphism-sheaf condition used below. Thus
 399: any later notation that writes the coefficient group as $A$ on nonempty
 400: basic objects abbreviates sections of the sheaf $A_{\mathcal U}$, not the
 401: constant presheaf with identity restrictions on an arbitrary site.

 403: 
 404: \begin{lemma}[Slice \(H^1\)-vanishing and neutral one-object stacks]\label{lem:formal-site-neutral-stack-h1}
 405: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
 406: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, and assume that every
 407: nonempty good-cover intersection \(|U_J|\) is contractible and that
 408: \[
 409: H_1(N(\mathcal U),\mathbb Z)=0.
 410: \]
 411: Let
 412: \(A_{\mathcal U}\) be the genuine constant sheaf associated with an abelian
 413: group \(A\) on the chosen good-cover space. Then for every object
 414: \(W\in\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\),
 415: \[
 416: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})=0.
 417: \]
 418: Moreover, on every slice \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\), the prestack
 419: \(P^0_W\) with one object \(*_V\) over each \(V\to W\), automorphism group
 420: \(\operatorname{Aut}(*_V)=A_{\mathcal U}(V)\), and restriction functors
 421: given by the sheaf restrictions of \(A_{\mathcal U}\), is a stack.
 422: \end{lemma}
 423: 
 424: \begin{proof}
 425: If \(W=\emptyset\), the slice has a terminal initial object with only the
 426: empty-cover contribution and \(A_{\mathcal U}(\emptyset)=0\), so the
 427: cohomology group is zero. If \(W=U_J\) is nonempty, the slice
 428: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\) is the formal good-cover site obtained by
 429: pulling the chosen good cover back to the contractible intersection
 430: \(|U_J|\). All nonempty finite intersections in that pulled-back cover are
 431: again contractible, and the strict initial object accounts for empty
 432: intersections. Leray's theorem for this acyclic cover identifies
 433: \[
 434: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W,A_{\mathcal U})
 435: \cong
 436: \check H^1(\mathcal U|_W,A_{\mathcal U})
 437: \cong
 438: H^1(|W|,A).
 439: \]
 440: The last group is zero because \(|W|\) is contractible. If \(W=a\), the
 441: same comparison gives
 442: \[
 443: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/a,A_{\mathcal U})
 444: \cong H^1(|N|,A).
 445: \]
 446: In the construction theorems that use neutral summands we assume
 447: \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\); the universal coefficient theorem then gives
 448: \[
 449: H^1(|N|,A)\cong \operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0
 450: \]
 451: for every abelian group \(A\). Thus the displayed vanishing holds on every
 452: relevant slice.
 453: 
 454: It remains to spell out why this vanishing, not merely the sheaf condition
 455: for automorphisms, makes the neutral one-object prestack a stack. Let
 456: \(\{V_i\to V\}\) be a cover in \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/W\). A descent datum
 457: for objects of \(P^0_W\) consists of the unique local lift objects together with
 458: transition automorphisms
 459: \[
 460: g_{ij}\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_i\times_VV_j)
 461: \]
 462: satisfying \(g_{jk}+g_{ij}=g_{ik}\) on triple overlaps. This is precisely an
 463: \(A_{\mathcal U}\)-torsor cocycle on the slice over \(V\). Since
 464: \[
 465: H^1(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}/V,A_{\mathcal U})=0,
 466: \]
 467: there are sections \(h_i\in A_{\mathcal U}(V_i)\) with
 468: \[
 469: g_{ij}=h_j-h_i.
 470: \]
 471: After changing the local identifications by the \(h_i\), the descent datum
 472: is equivalent to the trivial descent datum, which glues to the object
 473: \(*_V\). Descent for morphisms is exactly the sheaf condition for
 474: \(A_{\mathcal U}\), already proved in
 475: \Cref{lem:formal-site-constant-sheaf-banding}. Hence \(P^0_W\) is a stack.
 476: \end{proof}
 477: 
 478: \begin{lemma}[Nonempty refinements in the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve]\label{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}
 479: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
 480: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}. If \(W\ne\emptyset\), then
 481: every covering family of \(W\) has a nonempty member. Consequently,
 482: pulling any covering family of \(a\) back to a vertex object \(U_i\)
 483: produces a cover of \(U_i\) with at least one nonempty member.
 484: \end{lemma}
 485: 
 486: \begin{proof}
 487: The pretopology on \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) is generated by the vertex
 488: cover, its pullbacks, identity covers, transitivity, and the empty cover of
 489: \(\emptyset\). The only generator with no nonempty member is the empty
 490: cover of the strict initial object. Pullback stability does not turn a
 491: cover of a nonempty object into a family consisting only of
 492: \(\emptyset\): if \(W=U_J\ne\emptyset\), the pullback of the vertex cover
 493: contains the term \(\widetilde U_{J\cup\{j\}}=U_J\) for every \(j\in J\).
 494: Identity covers of nonempty objects are nonempty, and transitivity
 495: preserves the existence of a nonempty member because a cover of a
 496: nonempty intermediate object again has a nonempty member by the same
 497: induction on the generation of covers. Thus every generated cover of
 498: \(W\ne\emptyset\) has at least one nonempty member. The final assertion is
 499: the case \(W=U_i\) after pullback.
 500: \end{proof}
 501: 
 502: \begin{lemma}[Sheafification of the formal label presheaf]\label{lem:formal-label-sheafification}
 503: Let \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) be the finite intersection site associated to the cover nerve of
 504: \Cref{lem:generated-subsite-model-interface}, and assume that the
 505: nonempty nerve \(N(\mathcal U)\) is connected. Let \(\Lambda\) be a set and
 506: \(\Lambda_0\subseteq\Lambda\). Define a presheaf \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) on
 507: \(\mathcal C_{\mathcal U}\) by
 508: \[
 509: F_{\Lambda_0}(W)=
 510: \begin{cases}
 511: \Lambda,& W\to a\text{ a nonempty proper formal intersection object},\\
 512: \Lambda_0,& W=a,\\
 513: \{\ast\},& W=\emptyset,
 514: \end{cases}
 515: \]
 516: with identity restrictions among nonempty proper intersection objects,
 517: the inclusion \(\Lambda_0\hookrightarrow\Lambda\) from \(a\) to nonempty
 518: proper objects, and the unique restriction to \(\emptyset\). Then
 519: \[
 520: \bigl(aF_{\Lambda_0}\bigr)(a)\cong\Lambda.
 521: \]
 522: Under this bijection, a label \(\lambda\in\Lambda\) is represented by the
 523: strict matching family that assigns \(\lambda\) to every vertex object
 524: \(U_i\).
 525: \end{lemma}
 526: 
 527: \begin{proof}
 528: For every \(\lambda\in\Lambda\), the constant vertex assignment
 529: \(\lambda_i=\lambda\in F_{\Lambda_0}(U_i)\) is a strict matching family:
 530: on a nonempty pairwise overlap the two restrictions are the same label,
 531: and on an empty pairwise overlap both restrictions are the unique element
 532: of \(F_{\Lambda_0}(\emptyset)\). This gives a section of
 533: \((aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\).
 534: 
 535: Conversely, let \(\xi\in(aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\). By
 536: \Cref{lem:strict-sheafification-representatives}, after replacing its
 537: plus--plus representative by a composite cover, \(\xi\) is represented by
 538: sections of \(F_{\Lambda_0}\) whose restrictions to each pullback overlap
 539: agree after a further cover of that overlap. Pull this cover back along
 540: the vertex cover \(\{U_i\to a\}\). For each vertex \(i\), the resulting
 541: cover of \(U_i\) has a nonempty member by
 542: \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}; choose one such member and
 543: call the label carried by its section \(\lambda_i\in\Lambda\). This label
 544: is independent of the chosen nonempty member of the pulled-back cover of
 545: \(U_i\): two such members meet after a common covering refinement in the
 546: plus--plus representative, and on every nonempty refinement all
 547: restriction maps among nonempty proper objects are identities on
 548: \(\Lambda\), while refinements through \(\emptyset\) carry no label.
 549: 
 550: If two vertices \(i\) and \(j\) are joined by an edge of \(N\), the
 551: pullback \(U_{ij}=U_i\times_aU_j\) is a nonempty object. Pulling the
 552: representative further to \(U_{ij}\) and using
 553: \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements} gives a nonempty refinement on
 554: which the two restrictions agree in \(F_{\Lambda_0}\). Since the
 555: restrictions from \(U_i\) and \(U_j\) to any nonempty refinement of
 556: \(U_{ij}\) are identity maps on \(\Lambda\), this equality forces
 557: \(\lambda_i=\lambda_j\). The nerve is connected, so equality propagates
 558: along edge paths and all vertices carry one common label
 559: \(\lambda\in\Lambda\). Every nonempty piece of the composite
 560: representative maps to some vertex and therefore carries this same label.
 561: Thus \(\xi\) is the section represented by the constant vertex family with
 562: label \(\lambda\).
 563: 
 564: It remains only to check that distinct labels do not become equivalent.
 565: If the constant families labelled \(\lambda\) and \(\mu\) are equivalent
 566: in the plus--plus construction, then after a common covering refinement
 567: their restrictions agree on every nonempty member of that refinement.
 568: Pulling the common refinement back to any vertex object \(U_i\) gives a
 569: cover of \(U_i\). By \Cref{lem:formal-site-nonempty-refinements}, this
 570: cover has a nonempty member. On that member all restrictions among
 571: nonempty proper objects are identities on \(\Lambda\), hence
 572: \(\lambda=\mu\). Thus the displayed map
 573: \(\Lambda\to(aF_{\Lambda_0})(a)\) is bijective.
 574: \end{proof}
