+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) is weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) is weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) is weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

 Let \(F\) be separated on the slice over \(a\), and let

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) is weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+More sharply, stackification of a prestack is fully faithful.  Essential
+surjectivity on a neutral terminal component therefore gives an equivalence
+with the full neutral gerbe, whose object classes form an \(H^1\)-torsor and

+combined with it.  These are sharp structural consequences of standard
+torsor theory, not a presentation-localization result.

 \Cref{prop:standard-component-gerbe-package}(iii) literal on a common
 presentation.
 
+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) is weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form (formulation A)]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative (formulation B; not adopted)]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) can be strictly weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+
 \begin{theorem}[Presheaf-to-component-gerbe comparison criterion]
 \label{thm:gerbe-null-semantics}
 Let \(F\) be separated on the slice over \(a\), and let

+\eqref{eq:degree-one-uct-terminal-vanishing}.  The torsor therefore has a

+is precisely essential surjectivity, rather than equality of object sets.

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form (formulation A)]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative (formulation B; not adopted)]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) can be strictly weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+More sharply, stackification of a prestack is fully faithful.  Essential
+surjectivity on a neutral terminal component therefore gives an equivalence
+with the full neutral gerbe, whose object classes form an \(H^1\)-torsor and
+whose object automorphisms are \(H^0\).  The marked terminal statement in
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form} retains those unavoidable ambiguities;
+the pointed-atlas alternative of
+\Cref{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form} is recorded separately and is not
+combined with it.  These are sharp structural consequences of standard
+torsor theory, not a presentation-localization result.
+
 On a finite good-cover site, the connected-overlap hypothesis is what permits
 the ordinary nerve to carry the full constant-coefficient Cech complex.  The
 all-subdivision basis comparison identifies the generated site with the
@@ -67,6 +77,13 @@

 treated as input but does not construct that lift.
 
+A genuinely presentation-independent theorem would require localization over

+\subsection*{The terminal torsor obstruction}
+
+Let \(\iota:\mathfrak P\to\mathfrak L=a(\mathfrak P)\) be the
+stackification of an \(\mathscr A\)-banded prestack.  For a global component
+section \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), write
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-source-component-fibre}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ :=\{p\in\mathfrak P(a):[\iota(p)]=\lambda\}
+\end{equation}
+for the full subgroupoid.  Thus ``in the component \(\lambda\)'' means all
+source objects whose images have sheaf-component \(\lambda\).  It does not
+mean one selected source component when several source components acquire
+the same component after sheafification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Terminal torsor obstruction]
+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[
+ \iota_\lambda:\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+\]
+is essentially surjective, then it is an equivalence of groupoids.
+Consequently,
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+\]
+is a principal homogeneous set under \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), and
+for every \(p\in\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\),
+\[
+ \operatorname{Aut}_{\mathfrak P(a)}(p)
+ \cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A).
+\]
+In particular, if \(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\) has one isomorphism class, then
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+For \(p,q\in\mathfrak P(a)\), the presheaf
+\[
+ U\longmapsto
+ \operatorname{Isom}_{\mathfrak P(U)}(p|_U,q|_U)
+\]
+on \(\mathcal C/a\) is already a sheaf because \(\mathfrak P\) is a
+prestack.  Stackification replaces an isomorphism presheaf by its
+sheafification and therefore induces an isomorphism
+\[
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak P}(p,q)
+ \xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}
+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]
+Taking sections over \(a\) shows that
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.
+
+Choose a neutralizing object \(x\in\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\).  The standard
+contracted-product equivalence
+\[
+ \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\simeq
+ \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C),
+ \qquad
+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the
+isomorphism classes of objects of the neutral gerbe form a principal
+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the
+automorphism sheaf of every object with \(\mathscr A\), whence its group of
+terminal automorphisms is
+\(\Gamma(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).  Transporting
+these statements across \(\iota_\lambda\) proves the assertions for
+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be
+a singleton only when the group is trivial.
+\end{proof}
+
+The fixed band in \Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} is essential.  If
+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form (formulation A)]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative (formulation B; not adopted)]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) can be strictly weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+

+\label{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction}
+Let \(\lambda\in\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\) be neutral.  If the restriction
+\[

+ \longrightarrow \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)

+\]

+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)=0\).

+   (\iota(p),\iota(q)).
+\]

+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is fully faithful
+\cite[Tag 02ZP]{StacksProject}.  Its restriction to the full subgroupoid in
+\eqref{eq:full-source-component-fibre} is therefore fully faithful, and the
+assumed essential surjectivity makes \(\iota_\lambda\) an equivalence.

+contracted-product equivalence
+\[

+ y\longmapsto
+ \underline{\operatorname{Isom}}_{\mathfrak L}(x,y),
+\]
+has quasi-inverse \(T\mapsto x\wedge^{\mathscr A}T\)
+\cite[Chap.~IV]{Giraud1971}.  Isomorphism classes of
+\(\mathscr A\)-torsors form \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\), so the

+homogeneous set under that group.  The fixed band identifies the

+\(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\).  A principal homogeneous set for a group can be

+the band is not fixed, automorphisms of the coefficient sheaf can also act
+on the classification.
+
+\begin{theorem}[Essentially surjective marked terminal form (formulation A)]
+\label{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}
+Let a finite marking set \(\Lambda\) be identified with
+\(\pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)\), and put
+\[
+ \Lambda_0
+ :=\{\lambda\in\Lambda:\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)\ne\varnothing\}.
+\]
+Assume that \(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) is essentially
+surjective.  Then the component of the image induces a marking map
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-map}
+ m:\pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\longrightarrow\Lambda_0
+\end{equation}
+such that, for every \(\lambda\in\Lambda_0\), the fibre
+\(m^{-1}(\lambda)\) is a principal homogeneous set under
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\).
+
+More precisely, after choosing one neutralizing object in each neutral
+component,
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)
+ \simeq \mathfrak L[\lambda](a)
+ \simeq \operatorname{Tors}_{\mathscr A}(\mathcal C)
+ \qquad(\lambda\in\Lambda_0),
+\end{equation}
+\[
+ \pi_0\bigl(\mathfrak P_\lambda(a)\bigr)
+ \text{ is an }H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\text{-torsor},
+ \qquad
+ \operatorname{Aut}(p)\cong H^0(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)
+\]
+for every object \(p\) in that fibre.  For
+\(\lambda\in\Lambda\setminus\Lambda_0\),
+\[
+ \mathfrak P_\lambda(a)=\mathfrak L[\lambda](a)=\varnothing.
+\]
+Consequently the complete set of terminal prestack isomorphism classes has
+the form
+\begin{equation}\label{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \cong\coprod_{\lambda\in\Lambda_0}T_\lambda,
+\end{equation}
+where every \(T_\lambda\) is an
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\)-torsor.
+\end{theorem}
+
+\begin{proof}
+Every \(p\in\mathfrak P(a)\) maps to an actual object of
+\(\mathfrak L(a)\), so its component label is neutral.  This defines
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-map}.  Essential surjectivity of
+\(\mathfrak P(a)\to\mathfrak L(a)\) restricts to essential surjectivity in
+every neutral component.  Apply
+\Cref{thm:terminal-torsor-obstruction} componentwise to obtain
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-groupoid-form}, the descriptions of isomorphism
+classes and automorphisms, and the torsor fibres of \(m\).  A non-neutral
+component has no terminal stack object by definition; it can contain no
+image of a terminal prestack object either.  Taking isomorphism classes of
+the resulting disjoint union of full subgroupoids gives
+\eqref{eq:marked-terminal-disjoint-union}.
+\end{proof}
+
+\begin{remark}[The pointed-atlas alternative (formulation B; not adopted)]
+\label{rem:pointed-atlas-terminal-form}
+There is a different consistent formulation, but it is not combined with
+\Cref{thm:marked-terminal-torsor-form}.  If one keeps
+\[
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)\cong\Lambda_0,
+\]
+then terminal essential surjectivity must in general be dropped.  The
+replacement is only
+\[
+ \operatorname{im}\!\left(
+ \pi_0^{\mathrm{pre}}(\mathfrak P)(a)
+ \longrightarrow \pi_0(\mathfrak L)(a)
+ \right)=\Lambda_0.
+\]
+This pointed-atlas, or chosen-neutralization, formulation supplies one
+distinguished neutralizing isomorphism class in every neutral marked
+component and none in a non-neutral component.  It omits the other global
+torsor twists that appear in the stackification, and is therefore weaker and
+more presentation-relative.  When \(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), even
+the distinguished isomorphism class is noncanonical; when \(H^1=0\), its
+representing object can still have the nontrivial automorphism group \(H^0\).
+If \(\Lambda_0\ne\varnothing\) and
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\), there is no third formulation retaining
+simultaneously this singleton-per-label equality, terminal essential
+surjectivity, and the nonvanishing degree-one ambiguity.
+\end{remark}
+
+\begin{remark}[The coefficient-dependent threshold]
+\label{rem:terminal-torsor-coefficient-threshold}
+For a connected nerve \(N\), degree-one UCT gives
+\[
+ H^1(N;A)\cong\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A).
+\]
+Thus \(H_1(N,\mathbb Z)=0\) is sufficient to collapse the terminal torsor,
+but the coefficient-dependent condition
+\(\operatorname{Hom}(H_1(N,\mathbb Z),A)=0\) can be strictly weaker.  As far as the
+terminal clauses are concerned, the manuscript's integral
+\(H_1\)-vanishing hypothesis can therefore sometimes be relaxed while the
+singleton-per-neutral-label description and essential surjectivity are both
+retained.  They cannot both be retained when
+\(H^1(\mathcal C,\mathscr A)\ne0\) and at least one neutral label exists.
+The all-non-neutral case \(\Lambda_0=\varnothing\) is unaffected:
+\(\mathfrak P(a)=\mathfrak L(a)=\varnothing\), and terminal essential
+surjectivity is vacuous.
+\end{remark}
+
+The two preceding theorems are consequences of standard stackification and
+torsor theory.  Their purpose is to identify exactly the unavoidable
+\(H^1\) ambiguity in terminal isomorphism classes and the \(H^0\) ambiguity
+in terminal automorphisms.  They are not a localization theorem.  A
+presentation-independent result over arbitrary cover presentations would
+additionally require a full refinement localization and a proof of the
+resulting biequivalence; no such claim is made here.
+
